<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>21:T43c,<p><code>n != n</code> reads the field twice. Without safe publication, the reader may see the reference to <code>Holder</code> before the constructor&#39;s write of <code>n</code> is visible.</p>
<p>It might read 0 on the first read and 42 on the second, and the assertion fails — a check that looks impossible.</p>
<p>The class is fine; the <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Publication" data-def="Making an object available to code outside its current scope — storing it in a shared field, returning it, passing it to another class." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">publication</button> isn&#39;t. Making <code>n</code> final, or publishing through a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="volatile" data-def="A field modifier guaranteeing visibility — every read sees the most recent write — and ordering. Doesn&amp;#39;t make compound actions like &lt;code&gt;count++&lt;/code&gt; atomic." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice"><code>volatile</code></button> field or a lock, rules this out.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>2c:T4b5,<p>The <code>Vector</code> is created, used and discarded inside the method, so <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Escape analysis" data-def="A JIT analysis that finds objects never leaving a method or thread, so it can stack-allocate them or drop their locks." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">escape analysis</button> can prove no other thread can see it.</p>
<p>Locks on an object no other thread can reach protect nothing, so the JIT can remove them (<button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Lock elision" data-def="The JIT removing locking altogether for a lock object that it proves can&amp;#39;t be seen by other threads." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">lock elision</button>) or merge adjacent ones into one (<button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Lock coarsening" data-def="The JIT merging adjacent &lt;code&gt;synchronized&lt;/code&gt; blocks on the same lock into one, saving repeated acquire and release." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">lock coarsening</button>).</p>
<p>That&#39;s why uncontended synchronization is cheap in practice and rarely worth optimizing by hand.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>2e:T5d4,<p>With a single intrinsic <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Condition queue" data-def="A set of threads waiting for some condition to become true. Each object&amp;#39;s intrinsic lock has one, used by &lt;code&gt;wait&lt;/code&gt;/&lt;code&gt;notify&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">condition queue</button>, producers waiting for &quot;not full&quot; and consumers waiting for &quot;not empty&quot; wait on the same object.</p>
<p><code>notify</code> wakes one arbitrary waiter; if it wakes a producer when the event was &quot;item added&quot;, the producer re-waits and the consumer that should have woken stays asleep — a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Missed signal" data-def="A thread starts waiting for a condition that already became true — and its notification was sent before it waited." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">missed signal</button> that can stall the program.</p>
<p><code>notifyAll</code> wakes everyone so the right thread is among them; explicit <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Condition" data-def="An explicit condition queue tied to a &lt;code&gt;Lock&lt;/code&gt;, created with &lt;code&gt;lock.newCondition()&lt;/code&gt;. A lock can have several, one per condition predicate." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice"><code>Condition</code></button> objects avoid the problem by giving each predicate its own queue.</p>
2f:T41b,<p>A <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Bounded queue" data-def="A queue with a fixed capacity. When full, producers block — natural back-pressure that stops them from running out of memory." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">bounded buffer</button> uses one <code>Lock</code> with two conditions. Why is <code>signal</code> safe here, when <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="notify" data-def="Wakes one arbitrary thread waiting on this object&amp;#39;s condition queue. Can wake the wrong one if waiters wait for different things." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice"><code>notify</code></button> wasn&#39;t with an intrinsic <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Condition queue" data-def="A set of threads waiting for some condition to become true. Each object&amp;#39;s intrinsic lock has one, used by &lt;code&gt;wait&lt;/code&gt;/&lt;code&gt;notify&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">condition queue</button>?</p>
30:T62c,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">private</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> final</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> Condition notFull  </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> lock.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">newCondition</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">();</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">        IntPair old </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> values.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">get</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">();</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">        if</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> (i </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">></span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> old.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">upper</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">()) </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">throw</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> IllegalArgumentException</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">();</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">        if</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> (values.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">compareAndSet</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(old, </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> IntPair</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(i, old.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">upper</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">()))) </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">return</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">    }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>33:T47b,<p>Without synchronization, the compiler and CPU may reorder independent actions, and writes can sit in a processor&#39;s store buffer before other cores see them.</p>
<p>Thread A may effectively read <code>b</code> before its own write to <code>a</code> is visible, and thread B likewise — so both read the initial 0s.</p>
<p>Only a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Happens-before" data-def="A guarantee between two actions — if A happens-before B, B sees everything A did. Created by unlocking then locking the same lock, writing then reading a volatile, starting or joining a thread, and so on." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">happens-before</button> relationship, created by locks, <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="volatile" data-def="A field modifier guaranteeing visibility — every read sees the most recent write — and ordering. Doesn&amp;#39;t make compound actions like &lt;code&gt;count++&lt;/code&gt; atomic." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice"><code>volatile</code></button>, thread start and join and the like, rules out such outcomes.</p>
34:T5cb,<p>Publishing an object means two writes: the object&#39;s fields in the constructor, and the reference into a shared field.</p>
<p>Without synchronization, nothing orders them for other threads, so a reader can see the new reference before the field writes — an object that seems to exist with default values in its fields.</p>
<p><button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Safe publication" data-def="Publishing an object so other threads see it fully constructed: via a static initializer, a &lt;code&gt;volatile&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;final&lt;/code&gt; field, a lock, or a thread-safe collection." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">Safe publication</button>, through <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="volatile" data-def="A field modifier guaranteeing visibility — every read sees the most recent write — and ordering. Doesn&amp;#39;t make compound actions like &lt;code&gt;count++&lt;/code&gt; atomic." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice"><code>volatile</code></button>, a lock, a static initializer or a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Thread safety" data-def="A class is thread-safe if it behaves correctly when used from multiple threads, however their execution is interleaved, with no extra synchronization by the caller." data-book="Java Concurrency in Practice">thread-safe</button> collection, guarantees the reader sees the fields as constructed.</p>
35:T9fb,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">private</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> static</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> Resource resource;</span></span>
